ParlaMint-ES_2023-01-24-CD230124.u5	Excuse me a moment, Minister. Ladies and gentlemen, can we keep a little silence in the Chamber, showing a little education?
ParlaMint-ES_2023-01-24-CD230124.u6	Thank you very much, Madam President. Citizens not only see all this positive news, which is a reality, but - more importantly - they notice it. They are noted by the pensioner, who sees how his average pension is going to increase 160 euros per month in January 2023, unlike the 3 euros per month that would have raised the pension under the 2013 law. This means for pensioners who will have 1560 euros more per year, i.e. a fifteenth payment they will have so as not to lose precisely purchasing power in the face of rising inflation. They are also noticed by workers, especially those workers who receive the minimum wage, which now stands at 1000 euros compared to the 735 that they received when we arrived at the government in 2018. This minimum wage is mainly paid to women, domestic workers and young people. That is why the rise in the minimum wage is a fair rise, a fair policy. [[Applauds]] They are noted by the 3.6 million workers who were protected by the ERTE during the pandemic, who did not lose their jobs and returned to their business when their businesses' economic activity recovered; also by the million self-employed who received the cessation benefit, or by the thousands of companies who went to ICO credits to ensure that they could continue their business after the worst of the pandemic. They also note the measures that we have taken in the Government by families and businesses, which have already saved EUR 28 billion thanks to the Iberian solution, which some of you will recall that the bench on the right called otherwise. For the Iberian solution has meant 28 billion savings for Spanish companies and families. And so have the thousands and millions of Spaniards who have celebrated these holiday holidays without fear of being fired when they returned to their jobs in January. Therefore, our country's economic situation and our country's resistance to the war in Ukraine are reasonably positive. We have good data that are showing the strength of our country and our economy. We will therefore continue to take measures to protect the middle classes of our country from the uncertainties caused by the war. There was another way of acting and perhaps we have not yet forgotten it; there was a way of governing that included cuts, austerity and also defending the interests of the powerful and not the interests of the country's large social majorities. This Government has chosen to protect the middle classes, the working classes of our country, because now it is when they need to be protected by the public in the face of uncertainties. It is a just way out of the crisis; what we are looking for is a way out of the just, socialist, progressive and social-democratic crisis. That is our model and this is where it is shown that different governments are implementing different policies. It is not the same to rescue the bank without any consideration that temporarily tax the extraordinary benefits of the bank to protect the great social majority of our country. It is not the same to lower your arms and to accept with your head the cuts and austerity that came from Brussels that to beat copper in the European Commission to get solutions for Spain and for the rest of Europe that protect the middle class and the working class of our country. [[Applauds]] Therefore, as we are not the same, as it is not the same economic model that represents the right and the economic and social model that represents a progressive government, today the results are different emerging from crises such as that of the pandemic and, then, that of the war in Ukraine. It is within this framework that he presented the royal decree law that I submit to the validation of this House, with measures of extraordinary and urgent need to meet the needs of individuals, families, businesses and, in general, our country in a situation as extraordinary as the war in Ukraine is. The measures contained in the decree can be grouped into seven blocks, which I will briefly and succinctly explain to you. The first block contains measures to protect the family economy. As you all know, the VAT on food has been reduced from 10% to 5% in the case of oil and pasta, and VAT has also been completely eliminated in basic foodstuffs such as milk, bread, flour, eggs, fruit or vegetables. We have also granted a check of 200 euros for people with annual incomes of less than 27,000 euros, which we believe will benefit 4.2 million people in our country. The 15% increase in minimum living income and non-contributory pensions - i.e. the lowest benefits we have to help the most difficult people in our country - has been extended and the increase in the inter-professional minimum wage has also been extended until the new minimum wage for 2023 is approved. The second block includes measures to reduce the cost of housing at a time like the current one. The increase in rents throughout 2023 is limited to 2%, evictions to vulnerable persons are suspended when they do not have a housing alternative and a mandatory extension is introduced for rental contracts that end during this period so that they are automatically extended until 30 June of this year. The third block concerns measures relating to public transport, to encourage the use of public transport and also to reduce it to those who use it. Free allowances are granted for nearby, rolling and mid-distance trains, as well as free bus allowances for services under the jurisdiction of the General Administration of the State. The rest of the public transport which is not State competition will be subsidised by 30%, provided that the relevant autonomous community also applies a 20% subsidy, both for autonomous communities and for local entities, in such a way that 50% of the price for regular transport travellers will be reached. The fourth block, ladies and gentlemen, contains measures to tackle the rise in energy prices. Until 30 June the compensation mechanism for the electro-intensive industry is extended, which has had such a good result for our companies, and also the price cap for butane candy and liquefied gases. Until the end of the year, taxes on electricity bills are reduced and the supply of electricity, natural gas and water to vulnerable consumers is also prohibited. The fifth bloc includes measures to protect the sectors most affected by the consequences of the war in Ukraine. For example, the climb of motorway tolls throughout the year is limited to 4%. The 20 cents per litre of fuel are maintained for certain professional groups in the transport or agricultural sector, and for those who do not benefit from this discount on the price of the litre, a direct subsidy per type of vehicle is established for taxis, VTCs, freight transport, ambulances or buses, a direct aid ranging from EUR 300 to EUR 3690. Finally, direct aid is also provided for the fishing sector of between EUR 1100 and EUR 300,000 per vessel depending on the type of fishing vessel - national or international -, the type or the length. The sixth block - and I do not want to overlook the importance of this block - collects measures to continue helping the inhabitants of the island of La Palma to rebuild their island after the consequences of the volcano. We remain committed in the Government to those affected by the volcano eruption and will be there - I myself was last week - until the last life project that was affected by the volcano eruption is rebuilt. We therefore extended much of the measures already in place, such as, for example, the moratorium on mortgage or non-mortgage loans, the exemption from the IBI and from the economic activity tax, the ERTE resulting from the consequences of the volcano, the termination benefits for self-employed persons. [[Applauds]] In addition to the measures that we extend on the island of La Palma, we also set out some new measures. For example, material aid is extended to regular housing for persons who were not homeowners and therefore could not be beneficiaries. I am referring, in particular, to widowed women who were users of housing because their husbands had died; in the case of these thirty women - because they were some 30 women who had not been able to receive assistance - we have diagnosed this problem and we have given it a fair solution, extending to the usufruct the possibility of benefiting from aid for the loss of habitual housing. We therefore continue to work for La Palma, just as we continue to work for the economic and social sectors of our country. The last block of the royal decree law, the seventh, contains other measures among which he highlighted only two. On the one hand, the fiscal measures for those affected by the forest fires that we suffered last summer throughout our geography - in fifteen autonomous communities - and which will result in exemptions from IBI quotas, reductions in the tax on economic activities and also exemptions from the aid received in the IRPF. On the other hand, in terms of health, we are going to keep the VAT on surgical masks at 4% and we are going to protect, by way of an exemption, the tax on vaccines and diagnostic tests. This is the content of the decree law which I submit to you and for which I ask the favorable vote. As you can see, it is difficult to say that no to this list of measures that you are going to protect and that you are going to help our citizens, unless the reasons for not voting in favour of this royal decree law are to try to harm the Government or any other that certainly do not have the citizen at the centre of the concerns of the political group concerned. These measures should lead to a virtually unanimous agreement, such as the one that should also lead to the measures that we took in the midst of the pandemic and that saved so many lives. They should call this agreement patriotism, because when there are problems of this nature we should all act together, and also in common sense, because it has been shown at European and national level that these measures that the Government is proposing are functioning measures. Therefore, so that we have a fair sharing of burdens; so that we continue to protect those who need it; so that we continue to protect the middle classes of our country, businesses and the self-employed, and so that we go out and avoid completely the policy of fear that tries to tell us that the future will be worse, we speak of hope, of hope in a better future, in which we continue to work so that we have a more just country every day. That is why, ladies and gentlemen, I conclude with good news for Spain and I am sure that for all of you: 2023 will be a very good year. It will be a year in which Spain will make progress in economic, social, political, rights and freedoms. Therefore, 2023 will be a great year, starting today with the approval of this royal decree law. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2023-01-24-CD230124.u24	Thank you, President. Egun on. We already speculated that we would be faced with a new package of spending measures that was not contained in the general budgets of the State, which was intended to be adopted by the second scenario that was sent to Brussels, because the Government already anticipated or knew the persistence of the circumstances that had motivated the first measures taken by the war in Ukraine. A decree to reduce energy prices, now also food prices, moderate inflation and support the most vulnerable groups or sectors, extending some measures and establishing new ones. A decree that contains short-term measures that my group continues - and we continue - to understand as necessary and that they pursue, as I said, to help families and businesses, to mitigate the high prices not only of electricity but also of food, the great horse of battle. Faced with the moderation of general inflation - which falls below the European average, largely because of the energy measures adopted and, in particular, because of the Iberian exception - the fact is that the underlying inflation, far from being moderate, has increased by 7%, similar to the European average. My group will support the decree because it contains proposals that my group has been asking the Government on various occasions, for example, to maintain the main measures to protect vulnerable families and groups, and to ensure access to basic electricity and gas supplies. The 30% subsidy for urban and inter-urban public transport for all autonomous communities, which in turn improve 20% of the price, is maintained for six months, a measure which, in addition, reduces energy demand, pollution and is progressive. Specific aid is envisaged for the intensive gas industry, a line of guarantees for intensive gas and a reduction of 80 % in tolls for electro-intensive gas; the maintenance of a line of aid for installations or industrial sectors with high natural gas consumption, whose subjective scope is now extended to new activities, as requested, and which will also take into account energy consumption, as we claim in its day in order to make this measure more effective. The industry relief contract is also extended, as requested by a question we asked the Minister of Industry, and includes the gas cap mechanism for waste energy recovery plants using natural gas as fuel. The estimate of the fuel price for cogeneration is also included. However, here we doubt a little of its effectiveness. If we take into account what has been approved for the second half of 2022, it seems that this remuneration will not compensate for the losses suffered by this sector so far. The management of transport in knots subject to competitions for access capacity for renewables is regulated. My group has also always advocated and called for measures to be much more focused on the most vulnerable sectors of society and the economy; measures to really support those who are having a bad time, as the European Union has been calling for. The truth is that, in this sense, the decree is somewhat more progressive, somewhat more focused on some measures. For example, with regard to the 20 cent per litre bonus measure, which is no longer universal for all people, regardless of their income, but is maintained for the sectors most dependent on fuel prices such as transport, agriculture and fisheries. Perhaps this measure should also be extended, for example, to self-schools, because for them too fuel is essential for their activity. We will support the decree, but we will also move some doubts with certain measures because they are not so focused, because they leave certain sectors out and because we understand that the effect is going to be very limited. Measures that should, perhaps, be reviewed. The fall in VAT on food; a measure not targeted as the same tax rates apply, regardless of the level of income of the consumer, regardless of its purchasing power. A measure which, by the way, the Government at first ruled out when the opposition requested it because of the sluggishness it would entail for the State's coffers, because it was not effective and because it had little room for manoeuvre and would also widen the business margins of the distributors. This was said by the Government. What's changed now? A measure that should be reconsidered because it is not focused, as I said, on the most vulnerable, because part of the rebate will be transferred to the offer and because, in the end, it is very difficult to know or estimate which part of the rebate will end up in the hands of the distribution and which part will benefit the final consumer. A measure that is not studied or designed to reduce inequality, because precisely lowering indirect taxes is not a redistributive measure. A reduction that the last studies, like that of Fedea, already point out that, in addition, it benefits more people with high incomes. A measure that, in addition, and despite the high collection, implies a significant loss of resources in a country with a significant structural deficit, which has to issue debt to pay what it spends. A measure which has also not taken into account the impact that certain foods have on health because, although the types of certain products are reduced, the VAT on meat or fish is left out of the reduction, the latter being a fundamental element of the Mediterranean diet and whose consumption has decreased considerably due to its high prices. With regard to the €200 cheque measure, I would like to mention that the measure was actually adopted in July and is now extended to a larger group, since it increases the income threshold so that they can benefit, but it is excluded from the cheque to persons who receive contributory and non-contributory pensions and those who receive the IMV because their payments have been revalued and yet may be collecting very low monthly amounts and not exceeding those thresholds. More than 50 per cent of pensions in the State are below the inter-professional minimum wage and women pensioners also have very low pensions. A measure that, in addition, takes into account the income of all members of the household, but does not take into account how many are or their ages to, at least, perhaps adjust that amount of aid. A measure that, moreover, needs an agile and easy design because the potential beneficiaries do not get the information to the effect and in many cases do not have the means to request it. Maybe this could be better. Perhaps we should be able to design vouchers or targeted checks by income level or product level to help those who need it most, including expanding existing instruments, such as IMV, to avoid that risk of poverty and social exclusion. Finally, an underlying inflation that does not give rise to truce and, however, the decree does not provide for anything about inflation and wages. Perhaps, when it asks others to revalue it so that the purchasing level does not decrease or do so to a lesser extent, it could set an example and perhaps encourage it to revalue it, at least by facilitating the revision of the prices of public contracts when the labour force is the main cost that would allow companies to impact on the price the costs of the wage increase applied in the agreement and, moreover, by facilitating the adoption of wage agreements, avoiding labour conflict and ensuring the provision of the service that today is impossible by the Public Sector Contracts Act. We will, of course, support this new package of social and economic measures that was adopted at the last Council of Ministers because, as I said, it incorporates measures requested by my group, because its purpose is correct, it is to alleviate the negative effects of the crisis, but also, we must say, we are already witnessing the use of this package of measures by both of us as a marketing policy in the face of the May elections and what is really needed is to roll over, transform this economy...

ParlaMint-ES_2023-01-24-CD230124.u59	Thank you very much, Mrs. Mínguez. - REAL DECRETO-LEY 1/2023, OF 10 JANUARY, OF EMERGENCY MEASURES IN THE MATTER OF INcentIVATIVES TO WORK CONTRACTION AND IMPROVEMENT OF SOCIAL PROTECTION OF ARTIST PERSONS [[File number 130/000093]]
ParlaMint-ES_2023-01-24-CD230124.u60	We turn to the following item on the agenda: Royal Decree Law 1/2023 of 10 January of urgent measures on incentives for employment and improvement of the social protection of artistic persons. In order to present the royal decree law, on behalf of the Government, the second Vice-President and Minister of Labour and Social Economy has the floor.
ParlaMint-ES_2023-01-24-CD230124.u61	Good afternoon. On one occasion he wrote the Mexican poet José Emilio Pacheco: 'Only the tree touched by lightning / keeps the power of fire in its wood.' Sometimes adversity gives us that kind of strength and always, always, the defense of what belongs to us makes us manifestly better. Today I come to this rostrum of the Congress of Deputies to expose the contents of Royal Decree Law 1/2023. It is no longer a normative text; it contains in its precepts the sustained work of thousands of people, artists of our country, grouped in dozens of unions, associations and unions of professionals of cinema, theatre, dance, scriptwriters, technical personnel of sound, lighting, scenery, actors, actors, poets, musicians, singers, painters, all kinds of people. Thanks to you and to you, whom you accompany us today in the rostrum of this Chamber; thanks to you because this royal decree comes here today. We are here because you have never given up your rights and what belongs to you as working people. We are here because you believe in your work and the transcendental weight it has for our society, and thanks to this we have the opportunity to collectively write a new chapter in the great book of our labor conquests. Thank you very much. It has been a pride to have worked side by side with all of you, and I know that we will continue to do so in the future, because I know that much remains to be done. We also carry out a task that stems from the unanimity of this House and from the mandate of the Statute of the Artist, a normative framework that we are legally developing thanks to a model interministerial collaboration. My recognition also, mainly, for the team of the Ministry of Culture and Sport - Mr.Iceta, who accompanies us - and the Ministry of Inclusion, Social Security and Migration. Together and together, we have laid the foundations for breaking a historic discrimination, an area of shadow that was intolerable, away from this sector, from its ability to generate wealth and employment, from the protection and recognition of due rights. Culture, like love or joy, is among the few heritages that grow by being segmented, that grow by being shared. That is your work, creativity, culture, and this is also the reason for our gratitude. In order to guarantee the right to culture, we must take care of, stimulate and protect the working conditions and the material possibility of artistic creation, contemplating conditions of intermittence and seasonality which are so often inherent in the sector and which have motivated an unjustified difference of treatment with respect to the rest of the working people, which we now endorse in this royal decree law. The first of the recognized rights is a historical claim of the collective of artistic persons: the creation of a specific unemployment benefit for persons who develop artistic and cultural activities, which also extends to workers who develop technical and auxiliary activities to artistic activity. It is a flat-rate contribution benefit that can be claimed when it is not possible to access the regular unemployment benefit or when it is suspended, in which case a contribution less than that established for the regular unemployment benefit is required, with 60 days in the previous 18 months or 180 days in the previous 6 years. This is an 80 % benefit from IPREM if the daily contribution base is less than EUR 60 and 100 % from IPREM if the daily basis is more than EUR 60. This unemployment benefit applies a regulatory correction that eliminates discrimination against artists. I would like to remind you that there have been many unemployment contributions that have been lost because the ordinary contributory unemployment benefit was often unattainable for workers in the sector. The second of the specific tasks for artistic persons is the right to reconcile their pension with the development of paid artistic activities. It is compatible with all retirement pensions, including non-contributory pensions if they do not exceed the inter-professional minimum wage, and allows them to be reconciled with any activity of an artistic nature and not only with activities protected by intellectual property, as was the case so far. It is a guarantee of great importance that will allow the pensioner to maintain a creative activity in function, that is, of his physical or anemic possibilities. This therefore leads to a gradual retirement as well as enhancing intergenerational exchange and the use of experience. In addition, a benefit is added for retired persons, particularly in sectors of activity such as the artistic sector, whose pensions have usually accused this intermittent work. The third of the contents is the establishment of a system of reduced social security contributions for self-employed persons. The Royal Decree Law allows for a reduction in the contribution base in the case of self-employed artists with annual income of less than 3000 euros, which also substantially reduces the contribution to 161 euros per month. It is a considerable reduction since, as you know, the minimum amount for this year in self-employment is 234 euros. This is a formula that will allow low-income self-employed artists to be integrated into the protective framework of Social Security, which will improve their living conditions and also their work. The part that Royal Decree Law 1/2023 devotes to people working in the artistic and cultural sector is complemented by three major future commitments for our country. Firstly, we will set up a committee to address the reform of the list of occupational diseases in order to ensure that the risks which also have special features in the artistic professions are adequately reflected. In turn, we will set up a working group for the adoption of specific social security measures for artistic workers to fully match the contribution and benefits to the characteristics of the sector. Finally, a series of tax benefits will be established applicable in the year 2024 on the occasion of the 15th edition of the itinerant Biennale Manifesto, which will be a platform of great interest to enhance the dialogue between art and citizenship, as well as support to Barcelona as a city committed to creation and innovation. In addition, I must point out that, although it is not included in this royal decree law, this very morning an amendment to the regulation on the income tax of natural persons was approved in the Council of Ministers to reduce the retention of the IRPF in compliance with the tax recommendations of the Artist's Statute. In particular, deductions for artistic activities in public spaces will be reduced from 15 % to 2 % and deductions for performance of activities of low economic value will be reduced from 15 % to 7 %. I continue to explain the part of the royal decree that regulates the new framework of incentives for employment. The revision of social security subsidies and bonuses in the area of employment was envisaged, as you know, in reform 7 of component 23, and the bulkiest part of this royal decree law is devoted to this. Since ancient times, and from different levels, it has been pointed out that the system of incentives for employment was deficient and was not being used either to create employment or much less to promote stable and quality jobs. The framework of incentives for recruitment in Spain has been growing in flood for decades, setting up a totally unstructured and complex system in which it was difficult to warn about the collectives and the situations that are being promoted. The effect was that of the widespread decrease in social security contributions without having a relevant effect on the employment rate or the stability rate and without affecting the improvement of the employment situation of the most vulnerable groups. The high number of recruitment incentives in our country has for many years been a sad feature, as are the high unemployment rate or the high figures of temporaryity. The incentives ended up being a pitiful excuse to the European Union and to the citizenry, which seemed to indicate that something was being done against unemployment and precariousness. What we are now trying to do is to combat the dead weight of bonuses - recruitments take place without the bonus being involved in the decision, acting simply, as you know, as a reduction in costs - and also the so-called Mateo effect, which led to employment aid being received by those who needed it least, with consequent inefficiency from the perspective of public expenditure. Now what we do is follow the recommendations of the AIReF itself and the European Commission's country-specific recommendations to correct these effects. We therefore needed a coherent and efficient system of incentives, which involved scrupulously identifying the groups and situations worthy of encouragement, promoting, in line with everything we do, stable and quality jobs. It was also necessary to unify regulations, requirements and criteria, and that is what the present royal decree has done systematically and effectively, which constitutes a system of incentives which for the first time in our country's history can be called, indeed, a system because it is homogeneous and establishes, as you know, a very important thing, an evaluation mechanism. Incentives that do not work, no doubt, will be reviewed, not as was the case so far. Instead of calculating bonuses by percentages, as was the case before, we set fixed lump sums, which are more transparent and, above all, less regressive, compared to the previous model, in which the bonus was proportional to the amount of salary, so that the more wages were received, the greater the bonus, which caused regressive effects. The new incentive framework obliges the contract to remain in force for at least three years. It is a fundamental requirement that cooperates in the generation of stable and quality employment and that it is responsible for the public nature of the funds supported by the system. Firm principles of impeccability are also laid down. It is not only a question of meeting the formal requirements, as it had so far, but also of certifying commitment to stable employment and a healthy economy. And that goal of social responsibility has another important measure in this royal decree law, which is not to allow relocations. This will mean that, from this moment on, they will have to return all the amounts stopped entering with the surcharge and the corresponding interest of delay for the contracts made during the four years preceding the transfer of the production, if necessary, out of our country. Quality employment is also promoted in the present Royal Decree-Law because the new system is built to enhance in a consistent and clear way the indefinite and full-time work. The system is clearly structured around the indefinite employment contract, which is strongly reinforced both when it is an initial indefinite contract and when it comes from the conversion of temporary contracts. The full-time employment contract is also vigorously enhanced. This provides for a proportional reduction in incentives in cases of part-time work, but also prevents access to them if the part-time contract involves a service delivery time of less than 50 % of the full-time working day. The systematic nature of the new incentive mechanism has an important virtuality that - I think - must be highlighted. From now on, future rules on recruitment incentives must respect these principles of coherence and systematicity and, in addition, they must be integrated into the framework contained in this royal decree without deploying specific and disconnected rules. If you want to observe today the amount of rules we have to study to see, to gauge the level of incentives in our country, you will see that it is a really complex task. We have corrected this today, and from now on we must follow this methodology. It is also a new framework of systematic incentives in relation to other current labour standards that are reconfiguring the overall framework of employment policies. Thus, the new incentive framework is the essential complement to the new employment law under parliamentary procedure. Having already entered the exhibition of the beneficiary groups, I believe that the first one to which I must refer is that of persons working with disabilities. I would like to highlight the way in which this text promotes the work of people with functional diversity, maintaining the incentives recognized and strengthened by the special employment centres, but also establishes special mechanisms for the integration and reintegration into the ordinary labour market of people with functional diversity. The system of incentives for the recruitment of disabled persons has so far been very effective and therefore it was essential to keep it in its entirety, but it was also necessary to reconfigure and systematize the system of reinstatement following a permanent incapacity in order to enhance not only the reintegration into the same undertaking and the same post, but also in a different position and in a different undertaking when the worker was over 55 years of age. In this way, tools are introduced, I think powerful, to recover workers who are able to work, avoiding their definitive expulsion from the labour market, especially when they are older, and also to increase the labour and social integration of people who have gone through long illness processes that have already concluded. Specific provisions are also made for the termination of contracts for persons with disabilities in special employment centres which are now serving in a regular enterprise. These are forecasts that encourage the normalization of work and the real and effective reintegration of people with functional diversity. For the same purpose, subsidies are also provided for the recruitment of disabled persons to replace other disabled persons who are temporarily disabled. The framework of incentives for the recruitment of persons with disabilities is complemented by an express reference to the White Paper on Employment and Disability, which, as you know, is being held at this time and whose conclusions will be crucial for development, development and improvement. I think it is important to highlight the good design of the mechanisms for encouraging the recruitment of persons with functional diversity contained in this royal decree law. The maintenance and stable integration of persons with disabilities into the regular labour market has been fully achieved by recognizing and assessing their labour and social contribution without paternalism and without exclusion. This efficient design has been made possible thanks to the work we have done with CERMI and ONCE, which I would also like to thank today for their work and their presence in this forum and, of course, to thank them for the work they have done to improve the regulation that we validate today and their ability to reach agreements, because also here the dialogue has been successful in an important agreement on incentives for people with disabilities and which takes into account especially those who have greater difficulties in accessing the labour market. [[Applause.- The second Vice-President and Minister of Labour and Social Economy, Díaz Pérez, gives a kiss to the guest gallery]] In particular, persons with cerebral palsy, persons with mental health disorders, intellectual disabilities or autism spectrum disorders with a recognised degree of disability equal to or greater than 33%, also persons with physical or sensory disabilities with a recognised degree of disability equal to or greater than 65%. The second of the priority incentive groups in Royal Decree Law 1/2023 are women workers, and the second is that of workers with conciliation responsibilities. In this way, the commitment to gender mainstreaming, which is expressly set out in the Organic Law on Effective Equality between Women and Men of 2007, and also in the Treaty on European Union itself, is implemented. Following this principle of gender cross-cutting, higher bonuses for indefinite recruitment have been established for women than for men in situations that we value as emblematic. Let us give examples: the case of long-term unemployed women or the case of women temporary workers in the field who can see their contract transformed into an indefinite form. The aim has been to review the system to maintain and strengthen incentives for the recruitment of women in situations where gender stereotypes of serious impact appear most strongly, as I have also said for older people and, of course, for work in the field. I would like to highlight the configuration of bonuses for the indefinite recruitment of women in situations of special vulnerability, such as gender-based violence, sexual violence, trafficking in human beings, labour sexual exploitation and, also, in the case of women in prostitution contexts. This is a very broad definition of vulnerability situations, which will be verified in an appropriate and flexible manner through the accreditation report of the relevant public administrations. The system of incentives to promote reconciliation and also women's health during pregnancy and lactation has also been revised. They are incentives that serve a dual purpose, because they also have, for the first time, as beneficiaries of young people. It is a fairer system than part of a fundamental principle: maternity and paternity are not a cost for which employers must be paid, maternity and paternity is an eligible right for those who wish to do so. Now, there is an attempt to reorient bonuses to stimulate the entry of young people into the labour market, while at the same time ensuring non-regressive conciliation rights. The mechanism has been to provide bonuses for the recruitment of young people to replace the person holding a job who was absent due to conciliation or risk during pregnancy or lactation. I think it is important to stress that the substitutions for conciliation are very appropriate for the integration of young people into work, since they allow access to professional experience through work with decent working conditions, without enhancing precarious work, as has so many times happened in our country. Precisely, the group of young workers has a special relevance in the new system of recruitment incentives, especially those under the age of 30, who will observe that they are present throughout the text of Royal Decree Law 1/2023. One of the new incentives - and I think it is very interesting - is the bonus for the indefinite recruitment of young people with low qualifications attached to the Youth Guarantee system. This is a peer-reviewed incentive to promote vocational education and training at all levels, both in education and in the workplace. Facilitating first contact with the world of work is essential to create professional and vital expectations for people, to help them channel career and training paths that allow them to have hope in the future. It is therefore particularly interesting, together with incentives for young people without training, incentives that effectively promote their quality training. The incentives for formative recruitment have been revised in Royal Decree 1/2023 to accommodate them to the new formative contract - Article 11 of Royal Decree Law 32/2021, labour reform - but, in addition, specific incentives are provided to compensate the company for the training effort being made. Training recruitment should be able to generate professional expectations in students and should also serve to meet business expectations. The new training contract that was established with the labour reform constitutes the framework for meeting these expectations, and it was necessary to accompany it with a contractual incentive mechanism that would guarantee these expectations. This I believe we have reflected in this royal decree law. The incentive of training is supplemented in this rule by a specific bonus for research staff with a predoctoral contract. Science and research should be key future professionals for young people, and it is therefore necessary that pre-doctoral contracts in research entities have adequate benefits to encourage commitment to doctoral training in research entities. The main feature of this system of recruitment incentives is that it is strongly based on stable and quality procurement, with particular attention to the transformation of temporary contracts into indefinite contracts, configuring bonuses for a number of cases where this transformation is particularly valuable. Thus, the transformation into indefinite is improved when it comes to a relief contract, regardless of the size of the company. The bonus has therefore been extended to all enterprises and not only, as was the case before, to those of less than fifty workers, thus favouring generational succession and avoiding the amortization of retirement jobs. Bonuses are also provided in the case of conversions of temporary contracts into indefinite contracts or for incorporation as partners or partners in cooperatives or public limited companies by persons engaged in training practices in them. This is a special bonus that strengthens the social economy and also promotes training in cooperatives and in labour societies. You will see that Royal Decree 1/2023 devotes special attention to the promotion of the social economy. I would like to thank the experience of the Euskadi groups and also of the Group of the European People's Party, which have made very important contributions in this area. This incentive system incorporates, for the first time in recruitment, an unprecedented incentive to enable workers in a social economy entity to capitalise on the unemployment benefit in order to acquire the status of partners. It is an incentive that allows social economy entities to be strengthened with new capital and also allows more working people to enter the dynamics of the social economy. The system set out in this royal decree is complemented by specific incentives for specific groups that deserve our particular attention. The main group with the greatest difficulties of employability in our country are long-term unemployed persons, persons working in integration companies or persons over 45 years of age. Measures are also put in place to promote certain sectors such as tourism outside the high season or also in certain sectors of activity in Ceuta and Melilla. In short, the system of incentives for recruitment set out in this Royal Decree-Law is designed to contribute decisively to the consolidation of a stable labour market, committed to quality training and to the full integration of all workers. In conclusion, I should like to refer to the adequacy of the procedure of the Royal Decree-Law, to the adherence of the extraordinary and urgent need required by article 86 of the Constitution for the adoption of this rule. We are faced with a rule that, both in its parent content and in its secondary content, directly connects with the fulfilment of Spain's commitments to the European Union to comply with the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan. I would also like to point out that we are clearly faced with a rule of a structural and non-provisional nature which, of course, solves current problems, but which looks to the future and which I believe has come to stay. A feature which, as the Constitutional Court has acknowledged on several occasions, does not diminish its extraordinary and urgent need. This concludes, ladies and gentlemen. Royal Decree 1/2023 is a rule that guarantees and strengthens this stable and quality work path that, collectively as a country, we have initiated, but it is also a rule that enshrines one of our most valuable heritages: the work of artistic people. We are highlighting its important contribution to our country; contribution also in economic terms, but - they will allow me - above all, contribution in values and freedoms. Culture and artistic work must be a vibrant reason of state, a political affirmation, an indefinite contract with the future of our society which, with joy and pride, we come today to subscribe. The work of artistic people means saying in the face of silence, knowing in the face of fear and ignorance. We need that cultural work, that whole flow of effort and talent, because I am convinced that it broadens our democracy and because it makes us better as a country. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, with respect for the unanimity that we have seen in this House, in the Sub-Commission on the Status of the Artist, and thanking you for the previous tasks, in particular of the Minister of Culture and Minister Escrivá, I ask you to vote in favour of the validation of this royal decree law. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2023-01-24-CD230124.u69	Thank you, President. I left it on. We are faced with a real decree with two parts, of course, clearly different: on the one hand, the regulation of a series of measures to promote employment through subsidies in social security quotas and, on the other, measures that come to develop the Artist's Statute. With regard to employment promotion measures, we have to say, as the Vice-President has said, that in 2019 the AIReF has already carried out an evaluation of the effectiveness of the recruitment and entrepreneurship incentive system in Spain from 2012 to 2018, and it has already advanced that this period was characterized by a large regulatory dispersion and a generalisation of incentives to the whole population, incentives that have remained in force so far for the most part, but that, of course, if labour procurement has been modified by the labour reform to provide more stability for workers and reduce temporary recruitment, it is logical that a complete reform of the existing system of bonuses and reductions should now be addressed in order to adapt it to that new contractual reality of the labour market. All this, moreover, within the framework of active employment policies to improve the quality of employment, the professional promotion of the employed, the promotion of job creation in the social economy and the promotion of the recruitment of unemployed, especially the most vulnerable. Therefore, these recruitment incentives focus on indefinite contracts with priority care providers, long-term unemployed, over 45 years of age, young people and women, as well as the transformation of certain temporary contracts into indefinite contracts. However, minister, let me tell you that it is somewhat curious that this reform has been addressed through a real decree law, rather than including these measures in the employment law being processed and that it could have given rise at least to a greater debate about what we are dealing with today, and even through the preliminary draft law regulating these incentives developed by your ministry and which has now been relegated by this decree. In short, this royal decree law provides for a series of incentives to hire financed with reductions or bonuses in social contributions to avoid this current fragmentation and dispersion, placing, as I have said, the focus on the groups that have the greatest difficulties in accessing a job, as it cannot be otherwise. And, in turn, new requirements are added that companies must meet in order to be beneficiaries, including the corresponding equality plan, the maintenance of employment for three years and the prohibition of business relocation; amendments are also to be revised so that they can be assessed in such a way that they can be adapted and modified as established. We appreciate this because, so far, the bonuses have been the gateway to the labour market, but they have not been the gateway to staying in that labour market. I believe that this decree seeks to change this too, without forgetting that stability and quality in employment are only ensured through training, through training and in accordance with market requirements. Yesterday we heard - and today we have also done so here - that this decree runs counter to the employability of women because it modifies the bonus of maternity replacement contracts, which goes from a 100 % bonus to a fixed amount. It is not eliminated and, moreover, it is for a certain group. In this sense, I must say that in my group we do not understand it that way. Maternity is not a determining factor that limits our employability. The employability of women can be limited and complicated, that is, because of the lack of co-responsibility in the care of minors, and for this reason paternity and maternity permits have been equated, with equal and non-transferable permits, which are steps that help to eliminate these barriers in women's work careers. Now, if we understand that this barrier still exists, let me tell you that it is not going to come down with a 100% replacement bonus, because the truth is that you should know that these barriers are mainly based on sexist roles and stereotypes, and for this you need another series of instruments, starting with education and following co-responsibility and conciliation, in addition to all public policies in favour of equality. On the other hand, the decree contains three measures related to the Status of the Artist widely claimed, given the peculiarity of the activity that they carry out and that have caused problems of precariousness and lack of continuity of work, and that the COVID was also responsible for demonstrating. First, the special unemployment benefit for artists and technicians in the performing, audiovisual and musical arts, which responds to the intermittent nature of the cultural labour market and, in particular, the difficulties they faced in accessing unemployment during those periods when they were unemployed and the difficulty of having a number of listed days required to obtain the general benefit. Secondly, the improvement of the compatibility of the retirement pension with all artistic activity and not only with the artistic creation activity that generates copyright. And, third, a special contribution for self-employed artists whose annual net returns do not exceed 3000 euros. Measures all of them that we consider necessary, which, moreover, my group already endorsed when we signed those 75 points for an artist's status in 2018, and which gives, therefore, response in part or paves the way for that status. Yet, it is true, that there is still a way to go to address this specificity of the sector, a sector marked with many lapses of time without proposals or calls to work, in short, a sector with an intermittence that is not predeterminable, as with other discontinuous work such as work at sea or agriculture, in which, given its seasonal nature, it is predictable when it is going to be possible to work and when it is not. Having said that, and despite the fact that we are going to vote in favour of this royal decree law, it is necessary to warn us that there is no justification for the urgency of most of the measures, the clearest of which is the particular delay in the implementation of the measures to promote employment, which, according to the thirteenth additional provision, entered into force on 1 September 2003 and which should allow for their parliamentary drafting. We believe that nine months for its entry into force somewhat contradicts the extraordinary and urgent need. Finally, we understand that there are measures formulated with perhaps somewhat erroneous premises that should be corrected through their parliamentary procedure. I would like to give an example of incentives for the recruitment of R & D & i staff in a rule that focuses on vulnerable or low-employability groups, which may have distorted the sense of incentive in a way that is empty of content. Being Royal Decree 1/2023 a decree that already has in essence a negative impact on the bonus for R+D+i, by linking it to the hiring and activity and not only to the activity as until now, the exclusion that prevents bonus to a person who had a previous indefinite contract makes the incentive hardly applicable. Also, as regards the need for companies to have an equality plan in order to be able to benefit from the established bonuses, I would like to remind you that this issue is still to be resolved for cooperatives, because they cannot register their equality plans in the register established for that purpose and, therefore, they will hardly be able to benefit from these bonuses that are regulated today. Although my group has tried to solve this problem in the draft general state budgets, it is true that it, as I said, could not be dealt with because it is a non-budgetary matter, but we hope that it can now be resolved. Minister, we have nine months to solve this problem and thus extend to the cooperative world the possibility of articulating the legal recognition of the deployment of equality plans and enabling their public recognition in order to be able to join the measures, if necessary, taken by the various public bodies. Nothing more and thank you very much.

ParlaMint-ES_2023-01-24-CD230124.u83	With regard to unemployment benefit, we recognize the longer duration of the grant and the flexibility of access to the benefit, as well as the reduction of the necessary contribution days, in line with the intermittent work reality of artists, although there are still gaps, for example, for plastic artists or writers. The maximum threshold of EUR 3000 for applying the new reduced contribution is too low to be operational. This cap should be eliminated, as under the regime of street vendors, or, in any case, widened loosely. On the other hand, the share of 164 euros per month amounts to a total of 1970 euros per year, so the net profits, reducing - as has already been said - management expenses, subsistence allowance and travel, are minimal. We consider that the decree is improveable in the compatibility of retirement pensions with artistic activity with economic returns. There is also a lack of flexibility, for example, in adapting the retirement system for certain physically demanding careers, such as dancers, circus artists, etc. Finally, the Decree-Law is not sufficient in the area of taxation, and this issue has already been raised extensively. Companies in this sector also suffer from the precariousness of artists and there is not enough effort - we consider - to encourage business activity. In order to move forward in all these aspects, we will abstain and vote, instead, in favour of processing as a bill. Thank you very much.

Today, therefore, we are taking one more step forward, which we see as positive and which will improve the lives of many people working in the world of culture, a sector already in itself with many difficulties to survive and which was greatly touched by the economic crisis resulting from the COVID-19. This Royal Decree provides for the creation of a new contributory benefit for artists and technicians who are not entitled to other benefits. We could discuss whether it is generous or not, whether the amount of the benefit could be raised or not, or whether the days could have been raised, but what is certain is that it largely solves the great problem of intermittence in the sector, which was a historical demand of many groups and will now be a reality. In addition, this new benefit will be compatible with the perception of property and image rights, which is a subject that seems very consistent to us. We also welcome that one of the specific objectives of this Royal Decree-Law is to promote the recruitment of persons with disabilities, prioritizing those with greater difficulties and, in general, their transition to regular employment. In all this great project, which is the implementation of the Artist's Statute, we have always tried to help and be useful, both to the sector and to pressure the Government when it seemed that the necessary measures were not implemented, when we felt that measures were lacking or that some needed to be corrected. In this royal decree there are also measures that have entered through the negotiation of the Republican Group, such as one that seemed to us to be basic: the fact of being able to reconcile the collection of 100% of the pension with the realization of artistic activities, if desired. We have found some inconsistencies in the wording of some paragraphs, which may lead to errors in their interpretation or several interpretations, which could cause some defencelessness by some professionals in the sector. That is why we will ask for the processing of this royal decree law as a bill, in order to try to correct errors and to try to improve with more measures, if any, this decree. So, in short, we take a step forward, even if it is not the definitive one. We have a few months of parliamentary term and we still have measures for the cultural sector, so that it is a protected sector with the same possibilities as others and with a great future ahead of us. Finally, we would like to thank the whole sector, which here today has its representatives, for the work and effort they have been doing for a long time so that what we are doing today and other days before is a reality. Without your tenacity, this would not be possible. You know you can always count on Republican Left. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

[[Applauds]] The homeland is made with rights for citizens; not with hymns or flags, but with opportunities. Therefore, before those who only know how to wave flags, yes, today the Statute of the Artist, as we brought the IMV, as we brought the labor reform, as we brought the pension reform, the State Covenant against Gender Violence, the law of children, the law of euthanasia and, thus, up to 190 rules, we will do it again because it is as simple as this: or rights, or rights. We will continue to work for the rights of the whole world. [[Applauds]]
